## CONTRACT

| Requirement | Blueprint target | Current implementation | Status | Gap | Dependency | Priority | Verification | Work item |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R12-CONTRACT-9013 | Cognitive services exchange typed Rust Fabric contracts and own narrow state | Typed contracts in qip-contracts with a schema lock and compatibility gate. Within the central plane, services are composed in one in-process Platform in qip-kernel by design, so the seam that keeps a service out of another's state is the dependency graph: architecture.rs::service_crates_depend_on_each_other_only_along_the_edges_this_suite_names pins the thirteen service-to-service edges Cargo resolves and fails on a new one or a stale one. | COMPLETE |  |  | P1 | cd backend && cargo test -p qip-acceptance --test architecture -- service_crates_depend_on_each_other_only_along_the_edges_this_suite_names |  |
| R12-CONTRACT-9121 | Schema changes: compatibility gate, additive first, dual read/write, retire after inventory | A committed schema lock and compatibility gate run against the PR base lock: losing a field fails, gaining passes (tested). A reviewed per-(topic, version) consumer inventory, qip-events/consumers.lock.json (embedded in the binary), is read by `qip event-fabric schema-gate` (`--consumers` overrides it): a version the head drops from the lock is judged by ConsumerInventory::retire, so an unsurveyed version, or one a non-migrated consumer still decodes, is a finding; a bump that keeps the old row is admitted. The lock suite may hold a superseded row only while the inventory lists such a decoder. An acceptance sweep proves every crate whose source names a bound body is inventoried for its topic and every inventoried row's source names its type. Dual-read already exists (the envelope refuses a newer version and decodes older ones); dual-write is the lock carrying both versions during the window. | COMPLETE | None for the stated check. Stated limits, not gaps: the inventory is a reviewed file, not a computation; the acceptance sweep proves a lower bound (a crate that reaches a body by an alias or a re-export is invisible to it); no dual-read/write migration tooling is built, by decision (ADR 0156 1.8) because a tool that rewrites records between versions would be a second writer of history. |  | P0 | cargo test -p qip-acceptance --test event_fabric_schema_lock; cargo test -p qip-cli --test event_fabric_schema_gate |  |
| R12-CONTRACT-1195 | Signed model/strategy/belief/capital/risk packs flow to the reflex mesh | Signed capital envelopes, policy and halts verified by the cell; strategy plans and model digests checked; belief packs absent; not deployed. | PARTIAL | PolicyPayload slots are assembled in qip-api's mesh.rs, not in central/plane.rs, and risk_envelope is still Slot::unproduced there. Filling it needs a LimitSet -> qip_contracts::risk_envelope::RiskEnvelope mapping and a qip_edge::Cell apply seam that may only narrow the cell's limits; the edge side is the risk-and-execution domain, which asks for compliance_proof and security evidence and an explicit statement of the paper boundary, none of which this lane produced. |  | P0 | cargo test -p qip-acceptance --test downward_signed_path |  |
| R12-CONTRACT-3011 | Cognitive Core produces deployable policy/model packages | The kernel issues signed, versioned PolicyFrames and a ModelPack (model_serving::pack) by default; with a peer epoch live on the node a policy or halt reaches the cell only down the verified path, and nothing but the cell's own verification can mint a verified policy, halt or grant — the peer protocol cannot name one, so a tampered payload is refused. The check (the central plane issues a signed policy payload a cell accepts; tampering is refused) is met. | COMPLETE |  |  | P1 | cargo test -p qip-acceptance --test downward_signed_path |  |
| R12-CONTRACT-3117 | Typed Forecast contract for every material prediction | Forecast = Validated<ForecastFields> with deny_unknown_fields, constructed in production by qip-world-model lattice.rs and federation.rs, read by the kernel's lattice stage, and now also produced from a DemandForecast: DemandForecast measures its prediction variance split (aleatoric = the residual component; epistemic = the fit and leverage components plus whatever the dispersion floor added beyond the sample's own band, so the pair sums to (half-width / z)^2, the variance the interval encodes), states the interval's own tail at (1 - coverage) / 2 below its lower bound, one model with zero disagreement, and DemandForecast::to_forecast refuses a lane it cannot state (one reading, a band somebody else produced, an interval truncated at zero). The contract gains Stated<T> for exactly regime_confidence_bp and expected_impact: a measured value encodes as the bare figure, an unmeasurable one as {"unmeasurable": "<reason>"} with the reason required, at schema version 2; version 1 records still decode (Contract::reads) and cannot claim the arm. Forecast::state refuses an unmeasurable regime, naming the field. DECIDE reports how many of the cycle's forecast lanes the contract takes. CashflowForecast stays a schedule of dated, probability-weighted flows and is not made a Forecast (ADR 0159 G). | COMPLETE |  |  | P1 | cd backend && cargo test -p qip-contracts --test control_contracts; cargo test -p qip-capital-fabric --test capital_fabric; cargo test -p qip-kernel --test platform_outcomes |  |
| R12-CONTRACT-6109 | ForecastState contract: typed multi-horizon prediction | ForecastState (backend/crates/libs/qip-contracts/src/forecast.rs, Forecast::state) is built by LatticeStage::run for every forecast a pass issues and journaled beside the forecast in LatticeReview::states (kernel/lattice record on the world-model topic), which every cycle-running binary writes each cycle. The journaled state decodes strictly: a record with a state's confidence_bp removed is refused with 'missing field'. | COMPLETE |  |  | P1 | cd backend && cargo test -p qip-kernel --test now_lattice |  |
| R12-CONTRACT-6110 | WorldBranch contract: one plausible future | WorldBranch is a Validated contract with deny_unknown_fields; qip-twin futures.rs converts a branch to it (to_world_branch). | PARTIAL | to_world_branch has no production caller. The triage closure (call Future::to_world_branch in Platform::stage_simulate where qip-twin futures are evaluated) rests on a seam that does not exist: stage_simulate evaluates no qip-twin futures, and FutureTree/TwinState::fit have no caller outside qip-twin/tests/futures.rs (grep -rn FutureTree backend/crates --include=*.rs). Closing this row first requires growing a FutureTree in SIMULATE from the desk price series (TwinState::fit over bar closes, TreeConfig, WorldDriver selection) and only then journaling one WorldBranch per live branch; that is an M-sized kernel change, not the S the triage sized it as. |  | P1 | cargo test -p qip-contracts |  |

| R12-FABRIC-8074 | Broker uses bounded memory | The broker's protocol listener runs under qip_fabricd::data_plane_limits: the transport's ServerLimits defaults (64 live connections, 16 of them handshaking, one request per connection) with the body ceiling raised to MAXIMUM_REQUEST_BYTES. A declaration over the ceiling is refused 413 before any body is buffered; a connection past the cap is answered 503 at the accept loop and never reaches a handler. | R12-MESH-9181 | Mesh exchanges compressed state with bounded fan-out and message size | qip-mesh PeerLimits and qip-peer-link's node_limits bound peer frame size (MAX_FRAME 64 KiB) and fan-out (MAX_EDGE_PEERS), refused not truncated. Since 2026-10-18 the deployed centre link states its own bound: every half of MeshLink is built with centre_limits() (backend/crates/apps/qip-edge-node/src/mesh.rs), pinning max_body to MAX_CENTRE_RESPONSE_BYTES (4 MiB), so an answer declaring more is refused before it is read or allocated; a poll carries at most max_poll_batch = 512 frames, and the centre link addresses exactly one peer by construction. No raw tick stream crosses the mesh: the uplink carries CellStateDelta in the compact touch encoding. Compression is decided, not missing (ADR 0156 4e): no codec is admitted on the mesh or any transport, because a codec on that hop adds an inflate-amplification surface at the receiver, on a channel adjacent to the decision loop, to save bytes the bounds already cap. zstd stays admitted for tick-lake segments in qip-storage only (ADR 0113), and an acceptance scan pins that only qip-storage depends on a compression crate. | COMPLETE | None for the stated check. Credential expiry has no source on a paper platform (the simulated gateway authenticates against references to requirements, no secret material, and the venue credential is readable nowhere, ADR 0003) and is not built; the shape for a real-venue connection under ADR 0108 is a per-venue not_after instant in signed policy, recorded as unreachable, not pending. | R12-CAPITAL-4078 | Cash, stable-value, short-duration and idle-cash yield routing | route_idle_cash now takes an IdleCash that can be built only from the survival waterfall's report and what the cycle released (the opportunity capital the confidence governor declined to release), a validated operator-declared Shelf, RoutingTerms (share and ladder horizons) and the ReservationLedger. Each placement is a paper hold valid for the instrument's realisation, so deployable_capital sees it with no reconciliation, plus a DatedLadder entry; a hold parked in a yield instrument is left out of the gross ceiling (LedgerFigures::parked). InstrumentClass::Other is never routed and the shelf loader has no credit class; an instrument realised past MAXIMUM_RESERVATION_VALIDITY is refused at load. Platform::route_idle_capital runs once at the end of every DECIDE that built a budget, in qip-api, qip-fastbrain and qip-deepbrain, and journals a labelled paper record (YieldRoutingEntry). Nothing is routed unless a shelf with instruments and routing terms are declared. | PARTIAL | 2026-10-24 (re-read, unchanged since 2026-10-19; yield-shelf.json still lists no instrument): built, called from DECIDE in all three central roots, and unarmed everywhere. The committed data/datasets/yield-shelf.json declares no instrument, no routed share and no ladder horizons, because ADR 0159 leaves every shelf capacity, every realisation, the share of unreleased capital that may be routed and the ladder horizons to the RISK lane; no deployment sets QIP_YIELD_SHELF_PATH or mounts the file (a Cloud Run mount is a modules/cloudrun change plus per-environment tfvars under the protected infrastructure/environments/*; not attempted because there is no declared value to mount). Even armed, it moves only the capital the confidence governor declined to release, and the shipped schedule releases everything on a book with no calibration, disagreement, shift or regime signal (a plain whole cycle leaves nothing unreleased), so the share limit is the only lever on how much liquidity a cycle gives up. Routable instruments are those realised within the day a reservation may hold capital; the longer ShortDuration instruments the blueprint names need a longer-lived hold with its own record. Needs RISK to declare a shelf, a share and horizons, and a deployment to mount the file. |  | P2 | cd backend && cargo test -p qip-capital --test bank; cargo test -p qip-kernel --test capital; cargo test -p qip-kernel --lib idle_routing_tests |  |

Also not built, by decision or by absence: a retraction (a notice is superseded by id, never aged out, so a notice whose date has passed keeps refusing placements until an operator supersedes it, which is restrictive by design); the unfunded commitments' dated calls are not placed on the ladder view, because the router's one consumer reads declared notices (decision 10) and deployable_capital already withholds the whole unfunded balance. The grader's closure (build an intent per planned pre-positioning move in pre_position) was not implemented: a GateCommand carries three enforcement-point attestations that a human establishes out of band, and a machine minting them from its own plan would attest to itself. No single decomposition across the four. | PARTIAL | A four-way split needs three order quantities this platform does not stamp together: the thesis's reference size, the size after sizing, and the size the book finally allocated. AttributionLineage::note_release (runtime/qip-kernel/src/attribution.rs) stamps strategy, capital policy, risk rule, execution path and quoted spread only, and 'allocation' has no counterfactual yet (what weight the book would have held with no allocator). Without those, prediction/sizing/allocation can only be defined by choosing the numbers; the existing per-fill total (signed_qty x (mark - fill) - commission) already splits exactly into the execution term (arrival to fill, plus commission) and the held move (arrival to mark). Next step: stamp the three quantities at release, then split the held move as q_ref x move (prediction), (q_sized - q_ref) x move (sizing), (q_final - q_sized) x move (allocation), and assert the sum equals the fill total. |  | P1 |  |  |

| R12-LEDGER-5016 | Ledger has a treasury subledger | Account::Treasury is the credit side of two production operations that reach qip-ledgerd over ledger.operation_recorded: every desk's treasury funding, queued at assembly (Platform::queue_treasury_funding), and every capital grant's funding, queued when two operators issue the grant (Platform::issue_capital -> fund_capital_grant, OperationKind::CapitalGrantFunded). Both post Dr the book's trading account, Cr treasury:central; the trial balance closes in the acceptance test against a real ledgerd, and the treasury balance is the sum of both fundings. A grant's funding is recalled once — Dr treasury, Cr the book, for the funding's own deployable capital, as OperationKind::CapitalGrantRecalled with cause expired, revoked or superseded — by the cycle that observes its window has ended (Platform::recall_lapsed_capital_grants, ACT), by the ingestion whose recall order withdraws it (Platform::recall_revoked_capital_grant), or by the renewal that funds the same book under a later grant (Platform::recall_superseded_capital_grant, recall before funding), so the book's deployable capital at the ledger is the live grants' sum; the acceptance test shows a real ledgerd returning the book to zero and the treasury to the desk's own funding at expiry. A restart replays each funding and recall record as written, rebuilds which fundings are outstanding from the same walk, and the ledger answers each resend with a duplicate. | COMPLETE |  |  | P1 | cargo test -p qip-kernel --test central -- grant recall; cargo test -p qip-acceptance --test vertical_slice_ship -- funding recall; cargo test -p qip-contracts --test ledger_operation |  |

The producer is not built: the journalled CommerceSimulated record carries no warehouse on its purchases and no unit id on its sales, so the operation itself has to be journalled whole at the receipt and the sale seams in qip-kernel/src/commerce_research.rs and queued through Platform::desk_operations, which is a change to the SIMULATE stage's replay and was not attempted alongside the commitment and mandate producers. | R12-LEDGER-5026 | Continuous external reconciliation with warehouses/marketplaces | CommercePlane settles marketplace sales and returns against its unit ledger and, since 2026-10-18, reconciles them against the marketplace's own record: a SimulatedMarketplace books each settled sale and claws back each refunded return by its own arithmetic into a MarketplaceStatement, and CommercePlane::reconcile compares every settlement (presence, unit, net after adjustments, returned or kept) and every statement line (a sale the ledger never settled), recording each difference as a ReconciliationBreak and correcting neither side. The kernel's CommerceResearch::simulate reconciles once per SIMULATE pass and reports each break as a problem on that pass; it runs only when QIP_DEEPBRAIN_BREADTH_PLAN installs commerce research. | PARTIAL | 2026-10-24: a break is now acted on: an open break holds the units it is about (CommercePlane::sell refuses them, hold_on names the sale and finding, and CommerceResearch::work_listed skips and reports a held unit instead of sending it to a refusal that would end the pass); it clears only by a recorded correction on one side (restate_marketplace_line or adjust_settlement) followed by clear_break finding the sale in agreement, never by a later pass that happens to agree. Still open: warehouses are not reconciled, by decision (ADR 0153 decision 8: no warehouse keeps a record in the tree and none is built); the venue is not held, by decision; the kernel's skip in work_listed is not exercised by a kernel test, because CommerceResearch exposes the plane read-only and no break can be seeded through it; and the whole path runs only when QIP_DEEPBRAIN_BREADTH_PLAN installs commerce research, which no committed environment sets. |  | P2 | cargo test -p qip-financial --test commerce a_unit_with_an_open_reconciliation_break; cargo test -p qip-financial --test commerce a_seeded_dif |  |

| R12-DATA-9187 | GCS partitioned, immutable, reasonably sized segments; tiny objects compacted async | archive_names derives deterministic partitioned names; the segment archive writes content-addressed immutable objects (segments/sha256/<hash>) named by manifest rows that ArchiveReader::hydrate fetches by exact key and verifies. Compactor::compact now refuses, before reading or writing, any listing that holds an archive key (a sealed segment or a manifest row, at a path-segment boundary, so a configured prefix or an empty prefix cannot reach it), honours min_object_size (stored and never read before) and reports as merged only the objects it merged. Archive objects are never merged: that breaks per-key hydrate and the seal chain's hashes of segments; fewer, larger archive objects come from roll_after_bytes where the segment is cut (ADR 0156 2.1-2.3). | PARTIAL | Compactor has no production caller: nothing compacts the lake's small objects asynchronously yet, so the requirement's 'tiny objects are compacted asynchronously' half is a guarded tool and not a running job; it is wired when the lake produces tiny objects worth a pass. No test runs compaction against Cloud Storage. Note for whoever wires it: min_object_size is honoured as documented (objects smaller than it are left alone), which makes its 64 KiB default skip genuinely tiny objects; set it for the lake's actual object sizes. (Corrected 2026-10-19.) |  | P1 | cargo test -p qip-storage |  |

Resolved theses become Episodes in the same stage (remember_resolved). session_replay::replay reads the sealed ACT sessions (instruction against venue answer) and reports reconciliation breaks only; it prices nothing and has no predicted side. | PARTIAL | The record carries the residual in basis points and nothing else of the comparison: not the predicted price, not the actual price, not a slippage figure against the arrival, and not the twin's simulated P&L against the realised outcome. The closure proposed (session_replay::replay emitting a per-order Episode with predicted fill) cannot be met there because RecordedInstruction holds no price and a replay has no twin. Zstd writes checksummed frames, decoding is bounded at 512 MiB, and canonical_sha256 stays over the uncompressed bytes (lake_codec.rs:1-60). qip-edge-node main.rs:967 parses QIP_TICK_LAKE_CODEC into the drain (event_fabric/drain.rs:437), but the default is Identity, which stores segments uncompressed. qip-deepbrain tick_desk can run measure_partitions. | PARTIAL | Commit the bytes/day and drain-thread measurement, set QIP_TICK_LAKE_CODEC=zstd explicitly in the execution-node template and flip the default, unless the measurement shows spool pressure rising (ADR 0113 Amendment 1). | R12-TICK-2055 | P2 | a round-trip compression test |  |
| R12-TICK-2058 | Hot replay indexes separated from cold archive | Segment archive Manifest carries a replay_index of (offset, byte_start) used by ArchiveReader::read_indexed; fabricd archives sealed segments with the index. read_indexed re-proves the whole segment's content hash before it serves a batch, so it never returns unverified bytes. The per-batch digest format is decided and reserved (ADR 0156 2.4): a manifest wire version, replay-index entries gaining the SHA-256 of the batch's bytes as they sit in the segment, optional on read so every manifest written so far is served by the whole-segment path and never refused, and a ranged read as a new, separately named method whose result states what it proved (the batch equals the digest this manifest recorded), not a faster read_indexed. | PARTIAL | Ranged archive reads are not built, by decision: a ranged read trusts the manifest's digests exactly as far as the manifest is trusted, where the whole-segment path trusts it only for the hash, so it proves less; that weakening is stated and the build waits on a committed measurement of hydrate bytes and latency per read_indexed on the broker's replay path showing the whole-segment cost is the problem. Segment size is bounded by roll_after_bytes and the last hydrated segment is cached, so no such measurement exists. tick_hot_replay_indexes.rs must drive the real archiver (a separate test lane). (Restated 2026-10-19.) | R12-TICK-2055 | P2 | cargo test -p qip-storage --test segment_archive |  |

Every evicted episode is folded into an EpisodeAbstraction for its pattern: the count folded, the newest 32 member ids as lineage, the mean realised move over resolved members, and the known-at span. Abstractions are bounded by the store's capacity. With no action policy the engine executes nothing (Act is shadowed). On the deepbrain node (the default path), AmbientAgency holds a draft until a later sweep raises its subject again, then runs ObserveEffect, Attribute and Update through qip_agency::engine::learn on a measured outcome: the subject's reading at the follow-up sweep against its reading when drafted (status quo as the no-action baseline), kept in ActionOutcomeMemory as Observed::Measured, the Communication policy held to a predicted uplift of zero, the attribution recorded NotIdentified at identifiability 0, and the record journaled as DraftLearning (or withheld with the stage that refused it). | PARTIAL | none to build; an identified estimator is not wanted until ADR 0145 decision 8 is met (a control authorised by an ADR amending 0114, and a consumer). (1) qip-api has no Presence::Attested: auth.rs Presence has the single Unattested variant, Principal::presence always answers it and authentication_instant refuses, so nothing can mint an attested presence; ADR 0120 (accepted 2026-10-11) specifies it as an ECDSA P-256 WebAuthn assertion over a request-bound challenge from a POST /operator-challenges route, enrolled public keys baked into the image, none of which is built (ring is admitted by ADR 0110, no verification code uses it). (2) qip_contracts::action::check_authority has no caller outside its own unit tests, so changing it to take an Approval would gate nothing on a running path. (3) A qip-contracts Approval type cannot be minted only by qip-api (a public constructor can be called by anyone); the structural guarantee needs the attestation type to be unconstructible outside the verifier, i.e. defined beside the WebAuthn verifier and passed into the contract as an opaque token. Build order: the verifier and challenge route in qip-api first, then an attestation token in qip-contracts, then a Capital-class caller of check_authority. |  | P0 | cargo test -p qip-contracts |  |

Proving the call site needs a seam that lets a test hand reason_about_the_queue a ReasoningOutcome (or a synthesise that can omit the direct item), which is a design choice about test-only surface in platform.rs, not a fixture. |  | P0 | cd backend && cargo +1.94.1 test -p qip-kernel --lib a_thesis_with_no_supporting_evidence_is_refused_and_one_with_support_is_admitted |  |

(2) Broker identity is not mapped to a GCP service account/IAM: the bearer token is still the authorisation (tls.rs:38-41). (3) Policy keys are not KMS-held anywhere: Terraform declares no ADR 0110 policy/halt/envelope ASYMMETRIC_SIGN key (only the Binary Authorization attestor, modules/binaryauthorization/main.tf:57), and the default signer is still the shared HMAC key until 2027-01-31 (libs/qip-transport/src/trust.rs:99). |  | P0 | integration test |  |

| R12-OBS-8082 | fabric-observe: metrics/traces/health independent of own topics | fabricd serves health and Prometheus metrics on a separate listener; a test stops the data plane and health still reports. The data plane now records one span per produce at the request handler (DataPlane::finish_span), with the attributes stream, class and outcome and nothing else: never a partition key, a producer id or an event id, and a stream the catalogue does not declare is recorded as undeclared. The spans live on the broker's Telemetry tracer, exposed as Running::observability(); main spawns the shared drain on them when QIP_OPENOBSERVE_URL is set, and records them unexported when it is not. | PARTIAL | The spans leave the process only through the OTLP drain, and nothing is pointed at a collector (QIP_OPENOBSERVE_URL is unset everywhere; no collector is vendored or attested, the egress bootstrap has no listener for one), so no span has been shown to be ingested and there is no deployed scraper for the metrics either. Fetch, metadata and group routes are not spanned: only produce, the route whose cost an operator asks about first. The tracer's ring is capped at ten thousand spans and drops its oldest. (Corrected 2026-10-19.) |  | P1 | cargo test -p qip-fabricd --test broker |  |

An acceptance check replaces the byte-identical pin: the exporter's entry point is defined in exactly one file in the workspace, and no manifest sets QIP_OPENOBSERVE_URL. Since 2026-10-18 a broker can also be drained: ManagedLog::release lapses the holder's own lease row (Memory and Spanner adapters), Broker::relinquish_leases forgets then releases every led partition, and the lease thread in qip-fabricd/src/lib.rs does so on every pass once Running::drain is called or a DRAIN file appears in the data directory, and never renews again, so the follower leads at once with no database time passing.
